I will present that as: \boxed{(7+5)\times\left(\frac{8}{2}-2\right)}.

I'll now produce the final answer accordingly.</think>We can start by noticing that 7+5 = 12. If we can “adjust” 8 and 2 so that they combine to give 2 (since 12×2 = 24), then we’re done. Notice that

  8÷2 = 4  and  4 – 2 = 2.

Thus, using the four numbers exactly once we can write

  (7 + 5) × (8 ÷ 2 – 2).

Step–by–step:
1. Compute 7 + 5 = 12.
2. Compute 8 ÷ 2 = 4.
3. Then 4 – 2 = 2.
4. Finally, 12 × 2 = 24.

So the valid expression is

  \boxed{(7+5)\times\left(\frac{8}{2}-2\right)}.
